Add end-to-end tests for file upload content analysis

1. Drive FileSelectHelper from the picker callback (MultiFilesSelected)
   through ContentAnalysisDelegate and the real files request handler,
   so files are opened and read; only the upload is faked. Cover allow,
   block, mixed verdicts and policy off, on desktop and Android.
2. On Android, also cover kEnableFileAttachedEnterpriseScanOnClank off
   and content:// URIs with display names, which the Android picker
   returns.

[glic] Use TabListInterface::GetHighlightedTabs() on invoke

When default-invoking Glic with multiple tabs selected,
MaybePopulateSelectedTabsToPin previously collected selected tabs by
walking TabListInterface::GetAllTabs() and calling IsSelected() on each
tab. On Android, scanning every tab and querying IsSelected() per tab
incurs O(N) JNI calls across the tab list.

TabListInterface now exposes GetHighlightedTabs(), which returns
currently-highlighted tabs in strip order in a single call on both
Desktop and Android. Remove the GetSelectedTabs() helper and its
TODO(crbug.com/584742644), iterate tab_list->GetHighlightedTabs()
directly with a null check, and update
GlicInvokeSelectedTabsPreparationTest to mock GetHighlightedTabs().

a7412b28b90594f092fd3ba1e3459c3a79f7da40 [ diff ] 
media: Skip MediaFoundationRenderer tests before Windows 10 RS3

Production MediaFoundation clear and encrypted playback already require
at least Windows 10 RS3 (1709) and 20H1 (2004). Running
MediaFoundationRendererTest and MediaFoundationRendererIntegrationTest
on Windows 10 1507 (10240) exercises Media Engine behavior on an
unsupported OS build.

Skip both test fixtures when base::win::GetVersion() is older than
`WIN10_RS3`, eliminating the Windows 10 1507 MediaFoundationRendererTest
and HEVC integration failures without altering production code.

[iOS][EG2] Add build-time EarlGrey header metadata extractor

Adds extract_eg_metadata.py, unit tests, and the
//ios/testing/earl_grey_mcp:extract_eg_metadata GN action target.

At build time, extract_eg_metadata.py scans EarlGrey and *AppInterface
headers to extract class/method docstrings, #pragma mark categories,
parameter names and types, enum definitions, and strict per-method JSON
Schemas into eg_metadata.json. It delegates core header parsing,
path filters, and docstring completeness validation to the shared
earl_grey_header_util module, and enforces hierarchy breadth caps
(Domain <= 20, Class <= 25, Category <= 35, Method <= 35).

[AutofillOnTyping] Add flag to ignore autocomplete=off

Regular address suggestions ignore `autocomplete="off"`
(`FormFieldData::should_autocomplete() == false`) on all platforms.
However, `MaybeFetchAddressOnTypingSuggestionData()` and
`BrowserAutofillManager::OnGeneratedSingleFieldFillSuggestions()`
currently require `should_autocomplete()` to be true before generating
`SuggestionType::kAddressEntryOnTyping` suggestions.

This CL introduces `kAutofillOnTypingIgnoresAutocompleteOff`. When
enabled, address-on-typing suggestions ignore `should_autocomplete()`,
matching regular address suggestions.

parent
df01f51f7914d6e489a787829e38f60c94880626 [ diff ] 
Searchbox: Skip Top Chrome host when no active tab

When a Top Chrome searchbox WebUI (such as `OmniboxPopupUI` on
`chrome://omnibox-popup.top-chrome`) instantiates
`ContextualSearchboxHandler` without an active tab or after
`BrowserDidClose` clears `BrowserWindowInterface`,
`GetActiveTabWebContents` previously fell back to the host WebUI
`WebContents`. With `TopChromeWebUISharedRenderer` enabled, that host
`WebContents` runs in `TopChromeWebUIProfile`, which does not register
user prefs such as `contextual_search.search_content_sharing_settings`,
so attaching `ContextualTasksWebContentsUserData` crashes in
`PrefService::GetPreferenceValue`.

Avoid falling back to Top Chrome host `WebContents` when there is no
active tab, return early in `GetOrCreateInputStateModel` when no target
tab `WebContents` exists, and initialize `input_state_model_` in
`OnActiveTabChanged` and `OnActiveTabNavigated` once a tab becomes
active.

2a86a0fca3b92666f8c15f73829849d26b08f7ca [ diff ] 
Restrict scanout backings to web contents surfaces

This change refines the heuristic for picking surfaces to partially
delegate compositing to pick only render passes that are likely to be
the root pass of web contents surfaces.

In https://crrev.com/c/7997261 we implemented a cache to avoid the cost
of allocation on FCP and tab switching. However, we noticed that the
feature WebUIReloadButton makes the reload button be its own surface,
which breaks the assumption our cache made that there would be usually
one surface (i.e. the web contents) embedded by the root surface.

We previously partially delegated all root passes of surfaces directly
embedded by the root surface.

[PageZoom] Notify PAGE_ZOOM_OPENED across Page Zoom surfaces

Record EventConstants.PAGE_ZOOM_OPENED when the user interacts with
Page Zoom via:
- Discrete zoom actions (+/- and reset) in PageZoomManager (AppMenu
  zoom buttons and Omnibox zoom popup buttons)
- Clicking the Omnibox zoom indicator icon in
  PageZoomIndicatorCoordinator
- Keyboard and Ctrl+scroll zoom shortcuts in ZoomController
- Default zoom and AppMenu visibility preferences in
  AccessibilitySettings

[Isolated Mode] Add Isolated Mode header art to disclaimer dialogs

Follow-up to crrev.com/c/8376464. The Clear Browsing Data interception
dialog and the History disclaimer bubble show the Incognito header art.
In Enterprise Isolated Mode, this art is now replaced with a dedicated
Isolated Mode header image (domain icon on a light circle), matching the
CEP Isolated Mode designs and the Isolated Mode New Tab Page. Incognito
is unchanged.

Current view:
https://screenshot.corp.google.com/image/10n0v176j8g18.png,
https://screenshot.corp.google.com/image/6cqa62gcl4lo8.png

[policy] Warn on invalid RestrictSigninToPattern policy values

RestrictSigninToPattern was registered as a SimplePolicyHandler,
accepting any string without validation. When administrators configure
bogus values such as "0" or "Login@NotAllowed", runtime pattern matching
fails and clears managed profiles, deleting local bookmarks and history.

Introduce RestrictSigninToPatternPolicyHandler and
signin::IsUsernamePatternValid to validate patterns via a semantic probe
and domain structure check. Emit a non-blocking warning on
chrome://policy for invalid regexes and bogus patterns while continuing
to apply the preference.

This change was written with AI assistance.

f2dcd1b0b07649e30a5a6b480ec6078706202e23 [ diff ] 
Remove auxiliary-search-donation flag and kAuxiliarySearchDonation

kAuxiliarySearchDonation is disabled by default and has no Finch config;
it existed only so the chrome://flags entry could override the Auxiliary
Search tab and bookmark donation caps. The bookmark cap param had no
readers, and the tab cap always resolved to its default of 100, so the
feature is replaced by a constant. The browser test now passes the cap
explicitly instead of relying on a feature param.

NO_IFTTT=ChromeFeatureList.java never exposed AuxiliarySearchDonation.

[Tab UI] Enable TabGridDialogTest naming tests on desktop

testTabGroupNaming and testTabGroupNaming_afterFocusNoTitleSaved were
disabled on Android Desktop because they rely on the soft keyboard.
Desktop assumes a hardware keyboard, so the soft keyboard never shows:
Done does not end the edit, and back closes the dialog.

On desktop, skip the steps that need a soft keyboard and rely on the
title being saved when the dialog closes. Phones and tablets keep all
existing checks.

Reland "Remove duplicate AggregatedRenderPasses"

This is a reland of commit a2f6bf44d4220f0dc3fa670091522234efe8e83e The
CL is unchanged for reland but it's landed on top of another fix for
duplicate render passes.

Original change's description:
> Remove duplicate AggregatedRenderPasses
>
> SurfaceAggregator is known to produce multiple AggregatedRenderPasses
> with the same ID when a surface is embedded multiple times. Viz draws
> the render pass more than once which isn't necessary.
>
> Add a workaround to remove the duplicate render passes. The render
> passes should be mostly the same, except the damage and
> transform_to_root_target are different. For damage we union the damage
> from the duplicate into the first render pass.
>
> For where transform_to_root_target is used after SurfaceAggregator is
> complicated. It's used with CopyOutputRequests tracked_element_rects,
> DelegatedInk to place the ink trail, and OverlayProcessorWin for
> promoting overlays from non-root render pass. These already use the
> transform from first render pass so nothing changes.
>
> Bug: 582442902
> Change-Id: Icce3c0a3f342f366c29f1f1ebb1c27bd508d4c89
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8759351
> Reviewed-by: Vasiliy Telezhnikov <vasilyt@chromium.org>
> Commit-Queue: Kyle Charbonneau <kylechar@chromium.org>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1742300}

20d5177b437511f9931e078aa4d3ed2ed43cc1c0 [ diff ] 
[InfoBars] Launch the Known Interception Disclosure infobar migration

The Known Interception Disclosure infobar migration
(kMigratedKnownInterceptionDisclosure of CentralizedInfoBarFramework) is
launched at 100% in Finch with no holdback, but its default in code is
still false. Flip the default to true so that clients without a Finch
seed (first run, seedless or enterprise clients, or once the study
expires) use the centralized BrowserInfoBarManager path instead of
silently falling back to the legacy per-tab delegate.

The legacy KnownInterceptionDisclosureInfoBarDelegate and its
ConfirmInfoBarCreationAllowlist entry will be removed in a follow-up
once this has baked.

ce028b8ed0a9c9e9294d06e67ea44b1329af2bf8 [ diff ] 
[NTP] Extract shared New Tab Page loadTimeData and Mojo handlers

Pure refactor with no behavior change. Moves host-agnostic pieces of
NewTabPageUI into //chrome/browser/ui/webui/new_tab_page:core so the New
Tab Page component extension can reuse them in follow-up CLs instead of
duplicating them.

1. core/ntp_load_time_data.{h,cc} (moved from new_tab_page_ui.cc):
   - ntp::BuildLoadTimeData(): the body of NewTabPageUI::GetLoadTimeData(),
     unchanged.
   - ntp::BuildNavigationLoadTimeData(), BuildBackgroundColorLoadTimeData(),
     BuildCustomBackgroundImageLoadTimeData(): the bodies of
     NewTabPageUI::OnLoad(), OnColorProviderChanged() and
     OnCustomBackgroundImageUpdated(), returning the dictionary that
     NewTabPageUI still passes to WebUIDataSource::Update().
   - ntp::AddDataSources() and ntp::AddThirdPartyCookieGrants(): the
     URLDataSource and WebUIAllowlist registrations from the
     NewTabPageUI constructor.
   - ntp::AreActionChipsEnabled(): moved from the anonymous namespace.

2. core/new_tab_page_mojo_handlers.{h,cc} (moved from new_tab_page_ui.*):
   NewTabPageMojoHandlers owns the NTP Mojo page handlers, factory
   receivers, ContextualSearchSessionHandle, module_id_details_ and the
   NewTabPage.Count histogram. NewTabPageUI's BindInterface() overloads
   forward to it.

3. Small helpers needed so :core does not depend on :impl:
   - NewTabPageHandler::IsManagedProfile() forwards to
     NewTabPageUI::IsManagedProfile(), whose definition moves to
     new_tab_page_handler.cc (it must stay a NewTabPageUI member because
     signin::IdentityManager befriends NewTabPageUI).
   - NewTabPageHandler::GetTrackedElementIds() returns the two element IDs
     NewTabPageUI registered inline.
   - NtpPromoHandler::GetNtpPromoType(): the body of
     NewTabPageUI::GetNtpPromoType(), using PassKey<NtpPromoHandler> instead
     of PassKey<NewTabPageUI> to get the UserEducationContext, so the context
     stays inside an allowlisted class.

4. Cleanup of code made dead by the move: NewTabPageUI's unused
   weak_ptr_factory_, theme_service_ and Android stub members, and the
   empty early-return block in ~NewTabPageUI(). The new_tab_page_extension
   target depends on :core instead of the WebUI host targets.

Why this is a no-op for chrome://new-tab-page:
- NewTabPageUI's constructor runs the same steps in the same order:
  NewTabPage.Count and session handle, RecordActionChipsAnyShown(),
  AddLocalizedStrings(), URL data sources, AddRequestableScheme(),
  3P cookie grants, Observe(), backgroundColor/backgroundImageUrl/
  modulesEnabled/browserPromoType updates, tracked element registration.
  The only difference: ThemeService is looked up when the page handler is
  created instead of in the constructor, and the page handler and
  OnCustomBackgroundImageUpdated() get NtpCustomBackgroundService from its
  factory instead of a cached pointer. Both return the same instances with
  no side effects: ThemeService is created with the browser context, and
  NewTabPageUI's constructor still looks up NtpCustomBackgroundService.
- Moved function bodies are unchanged apart from renames (members to
  parameters, ntp:: qualification) and the PassKey class in
  GetNtpPromoType().
- NewTabPageMojoHandlers declares its members in NewTabPageUI's previous
  order, and NewTabPageUI declares it before the custom background
  observation, so handlers are destroyed in the previous order. The only
  difference: the drive and calendar handlers and module_id_details_ are
  now destroyed after the observation is reset instead of before it; none
  of them touch the observation. The NewTabPage.Count instance counter is
  decremented in ~NewTabPageMojoHandlers() instead of at the start of
  ~NewTabPageUI(); it is only read in the constructor.
- NewTabPageMojoHandlers caches the WebContents passed to it instead of
  calling web_contents() each time (a WebUI's WebContents never changes),
  and gets the help bubble's TrackedElementHandler through a getter that
  makes the same TrackedElementHandlerDocumentSingleton::GetOrCreate() call.
- DEFINE_CLASS_ELEMENT_IDENTIFIER_VALUE(NewTabPageUI,
  kRealboxContextualEntrypointElementId) moves to new_tab_page_handler.cc
  with the same class and name, so the identifier's name is unchanged.
- The component extension's config provider calls ntp::BuildLoadTimeData()
  instead of NewTabPageUI::GetLoadTimeData(); same arguments, same body.
- Tests call ntp::BuildLoadTimeData() with the arguments they passed to
  NewTabPageUI::GetLoadTimeData().

29b89802fa9ed7b877f55c3d54be81fbb53bc6e1
parent
f61230de777e4b41b72159f6a2f44288049275c6 [ diff ] 
[APC] Generate the new password in GlicPasswordChangeActuator

Replaces PasswordChangeActuator::GetGeneratedPassword() with
GetOrGenerateNewPassword(). Callers that only need the already generated
password pass a null form manager. PasswordChangeDelegateImpl uses it to
provide the new password to the change password tool.

GlicPasswordChangeActuator implements it: if no password was generated
yet, it generates one for the change password form observed by the given
PasswordFormManager, respecting the crowdsourced password requirements
of its new password field (or of the current password field if there is
no new one), and saves it as a backup of the credential being changed so
that it isn't lost if the website accepts it but Chrome doesn't save it.
A clone of the form manager is kept to promote the backup password
later. ScriptPasswordChangeActuator generates the password itself once
the form is found, so it doesn't support generating it on request.

Avoid invalid overscrollarea element types.

We want overscrollarea elements to become out-of-flow positioned
elements inside an ::internal-overscroll-area-parent pseudo-element. But
this won't work for all element types. BR elements, for instance, won't
do that.

When attaching the layout tree for a overscroll area, verify that it
becomes a LayoutBox, or just detach it again.

It may be that this solves crbug.com/582392018

[Extensions] Grant the New Tab Page extension its WebUI origins

The New Tab Page component extension needs the WebUI origins that
chrome://new-tab-page uses. Add them to GetAllowedWebUIOrigins().

Grants added:
- chrome://favicon2/ (subresources): an opt-in through
  "host_permissions", like chrome://image/. Declared in the New Tab Page
  extension's manifest.
- chrome-untrusted://new-tab-page/ and
  chrome-untrusted://ntp-microsoft-auth/ (navigations, New Tab Page
  extension only): granted by a new extension ID gate, since URLPattern
  can't express chrome-untrusted://. The embedded sources must also
  allow the extension as a frame ancestor.

Two static_asserts check the new entries: ID gates need a component
audience (so an unpacked extension that claims the ID with a manifest
"key" doesn't qualify), and chrome-untrusted:// entries can only be
navigation grants.

PostActivateExtension() now registers the chrome://favicon2/ and
chrome://image/ data sources for extensions that GetAllowedWebUIOrigins()
grants access to them, the same way it registers chrome://favicon/ and
chrome://theme/. This replaces the SanitizedImageSource registration in
NewTabPageExtensionBridge.

This replaces crrev.com/c/8749083, which granted all of
chrome-untrusted:// to every component extension.

Prevent CacheStorageSideDataWriter leak to sandboxed workers
📋 Metadata
commit
7f00dcab49769997c8e7870d4385b2af18bb6f49

tree
69ea225e5baf19826518163f0db10add089249e7
parent
0daebe0ac6336952172bd5f0dea4fe4d1ae05cf7 [ diff ] 
Prevent CacheStorageSideDataWriter leak to sandboxed workers

This CL prevents sandboxed or opaque-origin workers from intercepting
same-site ServiceWorker registrations or receiving a
CacheStorageSideDataWriter capability for an origin's CacheStorage
entries. It skips ServiceWorker main-resource interception when a
worker has an opaque StorageKey origin or its process cannot access data
for that origin, strips the side-data writer in the worker hosts when a
worker commits with an opaque origin (a CSP sandbox header without
allow-same-origin, or an opaque creator), refuses to forward the writer
from ServiceWorkerMainResourceLoader to a worker process that cannot
access the client's origin, and derives an opaque StorageKey origin for
nested workers created by a CSP-sandboxed dedicated worker.

Previously, a worker script request initiated from a sandboxed context
or served with a CSP sandbox header could still match the precursor
origin's ServiceWorker or retain the CacheStorageSideDataWriter pipe on
the main-script response head, allowing a sandboxed context to write V8
code-cache side data to the origin's CacheStorage entry.

Nested workers created by a CSP-sandboxed dedicated worker now get an
opaque StorageKey instead of inheriting the precursor origin's key, so
they are partitioned like workers created by a sandboxed document.

The side-data writer strip is intentionally not behind
kServiceWorkerDropHandleForCSPSandboxedWorker, which only guards the
web-observable ServiceWorker handle drop.

[WebAuthn] Remove obsolete BLE PIN TODO in PIN entry view

to reuse the view for. Folding the view into a sheet model doesn't fit
the current split of views and models either: the view's only user is
AuthenticatorClientPinEntrySheetView, which owns it as its step-specific
content, as other sheet views do. No bug tracks this TODO, so remove
it.

parent
7d1242a5d2418017c5d7a5efc10c3f575e49243c [ diff ] 
[Passwords] Guard password change toast lifetime

Updating or displaying PasswordChangeToast fires synchronous native
accessibility events (such as text changes and alert announcements). On
Windows, synchronous UIA event dispatch spins the UI-thread COM STA
message pump, allowing an assistive technology to re-entrantly close the
hosting tab. Closing the tab synchronously destroys both the toast
widget and PasswordChangeUIController while UpdateLayout(), ShowToast(),
ShowDialog(), or UpdateState() are still on the stack.

Store callbacks and schedule auto-dismiss before mutating child views in
PasswordChangeToast::UpdateLayout(), and check WeakPtr liveness across
event-raising updates in PasswordChangeToast and
PasswordChangeUIController as well as originator liveness in
PasswordChangeDelegateImpl.

Bug: 584267569
Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=*PasswordChange*Test.*

[DC] Use WeakReference in Android DC delegates

Previously, DigitalCredentialsPresentationDelegate and
DigitalCredentialsCreationDelegate captured a strong reference to
WindowAndroid across asynchronous GMS credential callbacks and did not
check WindowAndroid.isDestroyed() before launching the PendingIntent.
Capture a local WeakReference<WindowAndroid> instead and reject the
Promise without launching the intent if the window was garbage collected
or destroyed while the GMS call was in flight.

tree
564398d7f705e8b19ed315a783f24a1f0ec60c04
parent
0c11692d2b4ad81439bcd61b2983e755474ae12b [ diff ] 
Disable default apps in tests without modifying the command line

FakeWebAppProvider disables default apps by appending
--disable-default-apps to the current process' command line when it
creates its fake subsystems, i.e. while TestingProfile::Init() creates
the profile's keyed services, and
web_app::test::AwaitStartWebAppProviderAndSubsystems() appends it again
later. Other services may already read the command line on background
threads by then, e.g. DataTypeStoreService opens its LevelDB, which
calls SysInfo::IsLowEndDevice(). Modifying the command line
concurrently is a data race, which TSan reports e.g. for
ContextHubServiceFactoryTest once https://crrev.com/c/8630969 changes
which services get created.

Add webapps::DisableDefaultAppsForTesting(), which disables default
apps via a global bool instead, and use it in FakeWebAppProvider.
PreinstalledWebAppsDisabled() and
ExternalProviderImpl::CreateExternalProviders(), which only run on the
UI thread, check it in addition to the switch. Tests of default apps
which use a FakeWebAppProvider enable them again with
webapps::EnableDefaultAppsForTesting(). Also remove the now redundant
switch from unit tests which create a TestingProfile.

tree
5037b2316eb826771cd230078899e9b8629d5ab1
parent
f7485f2e367570cb04ce2feec76d5ba4a71ad399 [ diff ] 
Disable native window occlusion in sad frame browser tests on Windows

In `BrowserTestBase`, `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`
converts `Visibility::OCCLUDED` to `Visibility::VISIBLE` in
`WebContentsImpl::UpdateWebContentsVisibility`. On Windows,
`NativeWindowOcclusionTrackerWin` asynchronously updates the window
occlusion state as parallel test windows open and close.

When `CrashedSubframeVisibilityMetricsDuringTabReload` calls
`web_contents()->UpdateWebContentsVisibility(Visibility::HIDDEN)` while
`web_contents()->GetNativeView()` remains shown, an asynchronous
`WebContentsViewAura::OnWindowOcclusionChanged` callback recomputes
visibility from the `aura::Window` and calls
`UpdateWebContentsVisibility(Visibility::VISIBLE)` before the subframe
process crash completes. As a result, `NavigationControllerImpl` does
not mark the tab as `NeedsReload()`, and showing the tab fails to start
a reload.

Disable `features::kCalculateNativeWinOcclusion` on Windows in
`SitePerProcessBrowserTestWithoutSadFrameTabReload` and
`SitePerProcessBrowserTestWithSadFrameTabReload`.

Test History: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium%3Aci/%3A%2F%2Fcontent%2Ftest%5C%3Acontent_browsertests%21gtest%3A%3ASitePerProcessBrowserTestWithSadFrameTabReload%23CrashedSubframeVisibilityMetricsDuringTabReload%2FAll.1?q=VHASH%3A9b2dfe2002a10d6d

tree
24a7186a81f19b8618467535c80a4045979faef0
parent
a5ea51c3000ca316824c08b3b7bb63f50ef055eb [ diff ] 
[PageActions] Only notify omnibox suppression changes that affect visibility

When focus enters or leaves the location bar,
PageActionControllerImpl::SetShouldHidePageActions() flips
kIsSuppressedByOmnibox on every page action model. Each model notified
its observers, so every PageActionView re-ran OnPageActionModelChanged
even though nearly all actions are hidden before and after the flip. In
a local linux-chromeos trace this was 32 view updates per focus change,
synchronously inside the Ash mouse press dispatch that feeds
Ash.EventLatency.MousePressed.TotalLatency.

Observers only derive state from GetVisible(), and the suppression state
itself is pulled on demand (IsPageActionSuppressedByOmnibox and the chip
selector's is_suppressed callback). Behind
kPageActionsFocusChangeOptimizations (added with a
fieldtrial_testing_config.json entry), skip NotifyChange when the
suppression change does not change visibility.

641160e24b183ce69f57a5ef6d67d5345e65b5d2

tree
92c43e05bd4c284a2910af47f9fdcd5e2100846b
parent
571e1ffcd38423f0da253cf66da4b7fbf71f56d9 [ diff ] 
[WebOTP] Enforce Permissions Policy in the browser

Previously, only Blink checked the "otp-credentials" Permissions Policy
before a frame used WebOTP. The browser bound WebOTPService for any
frame that requested it, so a compromised renderer could use WebOTP in a
frame where the embedder did not allow it.

Now, the browser also checks the policy when it binds WebOTPService, and
terminates the renderer if the policy is disabled for the frame. This
matches other browser interfaces that are gated by Permissions Policy,
e.g. PictureInPictureService. Frames that are allowed to use WebOTP are
not affected, since Blink requests WebOTPService only after its own
check passes. The SMS origin matching and the user consent prompt stay
the same.

Fenced frames need no separate check since they have been removed.

This adds BindWebOTPService() to browser_interface_binders.cc for the
check, and the BIBI_BIND_WEBOTP_SERVICE_BLOCKED_BY_PERMISSIONS_POLICY
bad message reason. This also adds browser tests that bind WebOTPService
from cross-origin iframes with and without the policy.

Fixed: 497391442
Test: content_browsertests --gtest_filter=Sms*BrowserTest.*

Preserve dangling markup in serialized URL attributes
📋 Metadata
commit
836eea4697ed31f51f4c654efbc5d753e0c8a217
[ log ]
author

Fri Nov 13 19:24:59 2026
committer

Fri Nov 13 19:24:59 2026
tree
817bdc1680c203e69e0e941ceeacb7d4980f0f94
parent
41ed90601fba76b9bb7f719f6b6538eea67df10d [ diff ] 
Preserve dangling markup in serialized URL attributes

Preserve raw URL attribute values during resolving markup serialization
and paste-fragment URL completion when the resolved URL is flagged as
potentially dangling markup, matching the raw-spelling preservation used
by `CSSUrlData::MakeResolved()` and `MakeComputed()`. For marker-bearing
attributes, this keeps the original tab/newline and `<` characters
available to Chromium’s HTTP(S) dangling-markup mitigation on reparse;
flagged relative URLs may remain relative, while unflagged URLs and the
existing no-resolution/file-document exceptions retain their current
behavior.

When Firefox or Safari provides an already-canonicalized image URL,
pasting it into Chromium can still load the image because the original
markers are no longer present. Conversely, Firefox and Safari may
normalize and load marker-bearing URLs copied from Chromium, since they
did not enforce the same initial image-load restriction in the cases
examined; this change prevents Chromium’s own serialization from erasing
marker evidence, rather than providing a cross-browser clipboard
blocking guarantee.

7cabae48f337231524863aadb9f6d4abac9a78c6 [ diff ] 
[APC] Request original facet when fetching change password patterns

When patterned change password URL matching is enabled,
AffiliationServiceImpl::FetchChangePasswordURL previously requested only
the eTLD+1 facet and matched patterns strictly on it.

Include the requested facet alongside the eTLD+1 facet when they differ,
and check the requested facet's patterns before falling back to the
eTLD+1 facet. This preserves affiliation equivalence classes for the
requested facet and allows extended PSL domains to match patterns on the
requested facet even when psl_extension_list_ is uninitialized or stale.

parent
c31bb9cc03269c85b37db72a44b198b509fca8f1 [ diff ] 
[Multiprofile] Mirror the last used profile base name for use before native.

Code that runs before native is initialized needs to know which
profile Chrome will start with, but kProfileLastUsed is in Local
State, which only native can read. In this CL, native writes the base
name of that profile to the SharedPreferences:
- StartupData::InitProfileKey() writes the base name of the profile
  Chrome starts with, which is Default without
  AndroidMultiprofileStartupData. This also covers reduced mode, and
  fixes a stale base name, e.g. after a crash or a flag change.
- with AndroidMultiprofileStartupData enabled,
  profiles::SetLastUsedProfile() writes the base name of the profile
  that Chrome will start with (GetLastUsedProfileBaseName()), right
  after it sets kProfileLastUsed, e.g. when a profile is activated.
  Future callers, such as profile deletion, get this for free.
- Java reads it through ProfileManager.getLastUsedProfileBaseName(),
  which returns null if native hasn't written one yet. The base name is
  only a hint, so callers must check it against the profile Chrome
  actually starts with.

Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=ProfileManager*:LastUsedProfileBaseNameTest.*:GetLastUsedProfileBaseNameTest.*
Test: chrome_junit_tests -f '*profiles.ProfileManagerTest*'

[Passwords] Clean up kUnifiedPasswordLeakDialog

The unified leak dialog has been enabled by default. Remove the
killswitch together with the code paths it guarded:
- GetLeakDialogType() only depends on whether the password is reused,
  so the saved-on-this-site check is gone.
- The kCheckupAndChange dialog type and its traits are removed. The
  enum value is kept commented out since it is persisted to logs.
- The checkup dialog always uses the "change and check passwords"
  description, so the old checkup-only strings are removed.

OBSOLETE_HISTOGRAM[PasswordManager.LeakDetection.DialogDismissalReason.CheckupAndChange]=Replaced by PasswordManager.LeakDetection.DialogDismissalReason.Checkup after unifying leak dialogs.

[style] Use typed rule-set flags

Unsigned rule-set masks accept unrelated integers and are implicit and
error-prone. Use base::EnumSet to enforce the flag type and replace
bitwise checks with Has() and HasAny(). Pass the rule-set collection by
const reference to avoid copying it.

Inspired by the https://crrev.com/c/8564519.

tree
23b50bcc5cc944fc5d75da486ca55fd931cbf722
parent
cbc61fc235f07ffc8196605ffe80b8c90879f422 [ diff ] 
[remoting] Use 8 encoder threads at 1080p for AV1 and VP9

Lowers the 8-thread threshold in
`WebrtcVideoEncoder::GetEncoderThreadCount()` from `frame_size >=
2560` to `frame_size >= 1920` for tile + row-MT encoders (AV1 and
VP9). Caps non-Linux VP8 below 2560px width to 4 threads in
`SetVp8CodecParameters()` (Linux already caps VP8 to 2/4 threads),
avoiding CBR overshoot and PSNR loss at higher 1080p VP8 thread
counts.

At 1080p (capped at `(NumberOfProcessors() + 1) / 2`), hosts with >=
15 logical CPUs use 8 threads and hosts with 10-14 logical CPUs use
5-7 threads. On a 48-core / 96-thread Intel Xeon, 1080p delta encode
latency improves by 11%-37% for AV1 and 10%-27% for VP9 with
negligible bitrate or PSNR impact.

Adds table-driven unit tests for `GetEncoderThreadCount()` in
`webrtc_video_encoder_unittest.cc`.

tree
caec368e7c8e14272b1e16214c42af7d1474f4d7
parent
86cc99fad9973609b87abb9457575055fe002247 [ diff ] 
Fix flaky PageActionPixelShowAnchoredMessageTest on Windows

PageActionUiTestBase uses kActionShowTranslate as its test page action
and enables it on about:blank via EnsurePageActionEnabled(). However,
when the renderer for the initial about:blank tab finishes parsing
(DidMeaningfulLayout) asynchronously after test startup, it sends
ContentTranslateDriver::RegisterPage to the browser process.
ChromeTranslateClient::OnLanguageDetermined() then calls
BrowserCommandController::TabStateChanged(), which sets
kActionShowTranslate's ActionItem enabled state to false (since
CanManuallyTranslate() is false on about:blank), hiding the page action
view and closing any anchored message mid-test.

Detach ChromeTranslateClient from LanguageDetectionObserver when
EnsurePageActionEnabled() forcibly enables kActionShowTranslate for
testing.

Previously flaky test passed 100/100 runs:

Queue PaymentsChurnedUsersBubbleController confirmation bubble

PaymentsChurnedUsersBubbleController::ShowConfirmationBubbleView()
previously called SetBubbleView() directly outside BubbleManager, which
could show the confirmation bubble on an inactive tab and skipped
BubbleManager's WillDeactivate cleanup.

Use BubbleManager for showing the bubble instead.

Fix QuicHttpStream::IsConnectionReused() before stream creation
📋 Metadata
commit
4ce6260180dac78cc821024f89cec4c76a35b62f
[ log ]
author
Patrick Meenan <pmeenan@chromium.org>

committer

Fri Nov 13 19:14:03 2026
tree
782be1140bf72b6173f2ca7440ae941d8a27299b
parent
a333c26f76e781a3e9aaeed6a35ba4462ba535ce [ diff ] 
Fix QuicHttpStream::IsConnectionReused() before stream creation

QuicHttpStream::IsConnectionReused() returned false whenever `stream_`
was null. If a pooled QUIC session closed after a request was bound to
it but before InitializeStream() created a stream,
HttpNetworkTransaction did not retry the resulting
ERR_CONNECTION_CLOSED, even though the session had already been used.

When `stream_` is null, IsConnectionReused() now checks whether the
session was ever used to create streams. That state is cached on the
session Handle so it is still available after the session closes. Like
HTTP/1.1 idle sockets and HTTP/2, preconnected sessions are always
treated as reused.

7e72e0389c7fe4297c1f3593a0dce3b461bf9afb
parent
1faaafce0fd60452385ce04a9e284610babb3b4b [ diff ] 
[remoting] Use 64-byte SIMD alignment on AVX-512BW CPUs

Returns 64 from `GetSimdMemoryAlignment()` when `AVX-512BW` is available
(up from 32 on `AVX2`) so YUV buffer strides and `GetRowAlignedRect()`
widths satisfy `libyuv`'s 64-pixel alignment check for in-place I420 and
I444 `AVX-512BW` kernels. Active maps still use the unaligned dirty
region, so encoded bitstreams are unchanged.

On a 48-core / 96-thread Intel Xeon (`AVX-512BW`), `PrepareImage()`
latency improves by 8%-37% across VP8, VP9, and AV1 at 1080p/4K, with no
regression on small, scattered dirty updates.

Adds `ScopedSimdMemoryAlignmentForTesting` to cover 16-, 32-, and
64-pixel alignments on all CQ bots.

parent
a58066e7b97c20d3ddca7d581a55683aafd68d45 [ diff ] 
[TabSearch] Observe TabRestoreService to populate recently closed tabs on close

When TabSearchPageHandler was migrated from TabStripModelObserver to
TabStripApiBatchedObserver in crrev.com/c/7697541, OnNodesRemoved()
attempted to look up the TabRestoreService entry's SessionID via
SessionMappedTabHandleFactory::GetSessionIdForHandle(tab_id).

However, SessionMappedTabHandleFactory maps TabHandle values to the live
WebContents's SessionTabHelper ID rather than the newly generated
TabRestoreService Entry::id, and the TabModel is already destroyed
(freeing its TabHandle mapping) before buffered OnNodesClosedEvent
notifications are replayed at the end of TabStripService::CloseNodes().
As a result, TabsRemovedInfo::recently_closed_tabs was always empty when
closing a tab, preventing the closed tab from immediately appearing in
the "Recently Closed" section of Tab Search.

Fixed this by observing TabRestoreService via TabRestoreServiceObserver
in TabSearchPageHandler to record newly created TAB restore entry IDs
while the WebUI is visible, and passing them to OnTabsRemoved() when
OnNodesRemoved() processes the closed tab nodes.

[iOS][Signin] Support account capability overrides in signin-internals

Adds support for overriding account capabilities in
`chrome://signin-internals` on iOS, matching Desktop behavior for local
development and testing:

1. In `SignInInternalsHandlerIOS`, handle `overrideCapability` messages
   and delegate directly to `identity_manager->SetCapabilityOverride()`,
   matching `SignInInternalsHandler` on Desktop.
2. Refactor `AddAccountCapabilitiesOverridesInfo()` into a shared method
   on `AboutSigninInternals` (`components/signin/core/browser/`) to
   populate `can_override` on capabilities across both Desktop and iOS,
   eliminating code duplication between the platform handlers.
3. Enforce capability override restrictions via
   `CanOverrideAccountCapability()`, restricting modifications to
   pre-release channels (`UNKNOWN`, `CANARY`, `DEV`) and authorized
   accounts, matching Desktop behavior.
4. Observe `AboutSigninInternals` using `base::ScopedObservation` to
   refresh the UI dynamically when capability states change.
5. Add unit test coverage for `AddAccountCapabilitiesOverridesInfo()` in
   `about_signin_internals_unittest.cc`.
6. Clean up redundant code in `SignInInternalsHandlerIOS` destructor. A
   previous refactoring to `base::ScopedObservation` (b3537d8debeb) left
   behind an unused profile lookup, which was later mechanically renamed
   from `ChromeBrowserState` to `ProfileIOS` (a5c149f88d1d). This dead
   code is now removed.

Screenshot: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/2chlpdh4c9uag

Use base::fixed_flat_map and constexpr tables in ChromeDriver

Replaces runtime-initialized std::unordered_map instances with
compile-time base::fixed_flat_map in WebViewInfo and PromptBehavior. In
addition, moves prompt behavior and dialog type string constants to
inline constexpr in the header, adopts std::string_view for string
lookups and parameters, and avoids temporary vector allocations by using
static constexpr arrays of member pointers.

NTP: Use JniUniquePtr in NtpThemeCollectionBridge

Replace the raw long mNativeNtpThemeCollectionBridge field in
NtpThemeCollectionBridge with JniUniquePtr for type safety and
LifetimeAssert lifecycle checks on ::NtpThemeCollectionBridge.

Return jni_zero::MakeUnique from JNI_NtpThemeCollectionBridge_Init,
pass JniPtr to native methods, and invoke JniUniquePtr.destroy() in
destroy() before clearing mNativeNtpThemeCollectionBridge.

e0a59620eb8f9c4f820ed0c0cd59ca4a4cdbd53a [ diff ] 
WebID: Adopt JniRawPtr in IdentityProviderService

IdentityProviderService holds a raw long pointer to its C++ peer,
bypassing JNI Zero pointer validation.

Migrate mNativeIdentityProviderService to JniRawPtr and release the
borrowed reference in destroy() when the C++ object is destroyed.
After destroy(), the field is nulled, so any subsequent native calls
hit the null guards exactly like the old 0 and do not throw.

parent
8994ddf5cdf5fbf91827cb2de617664ea0326671 [ diff ] 
[ash] quick_unlock: hand QuickUnlockStorage prefs and a user hash

After the parent CL the only thing QuickUnlockStorage still wanted a
Profile for was prefs: once for the strong auth timeout, and once each
to build FingerprintStorage and PinStoragePrefs. The user id hash it
passes on is the only other piece. It takes both directly now, and no
longer mentions Profile.

The factory reads them off the BrowserContext it is given rather than
casting it to a Profile first. It deliberately does not go through
BrowserContextHelper::GetUserByBrowserContext(): ProfileSelections
here covers Ash-internal browser contexts, which have no User behind
them, and GetUserIdHashFromBrowserContext() is what ProfileHelper was
forwarding to anyway.

Omit cursor coordinates in DragSourceEndedAt when drag fails to start

fc7461e1d1af6c64e6db09694db5e36b8b955934

Fri Nov 13 18:59:09 2026
committer
Chromium LUCI CQ <chromium-scoped@luci-project-accounts.iam.gserviceaccount.com>
Fri Nov 13 18:59:09 2026
tree
f5054ff46336c91b826e9322ba95609a2ab4259a
parent
be5b2c9ea723205df03862dd4eed3f0ab2c32a19 [ diff ] 
Omit cursor coordinates in DragSourceEndedAt when drag fails to start

When a drag is rejected in the browser before starting a system drag
session (such as when the mouse was released, touch was not pressed, or
the view is hidden or out of bounds), querying the cursor position can
leak screen coordinates to the renderer.

This change makes the client and screen coordinates optional in the
DragSourceEndedAt IPC and WebFrameWidget interfaces. When a drag is
rejected prior to starting, coordinates are omitted from the browser.
Blink handles missing coordinates by falling back to the initial
mouse-down or last known pointer position when dispatching the dragend
event.

[e2e] Offset StatusIndicator below top scalp

Previously, the status indicator assumed no browser control layer sat
above it at y = 0. When the top scalp layer is present at the top of the
stack, this caused the offline status indicator to render underneath the
status bar with its composited layer clipped.

Offset the status indicator's Android view and composited scene layer by
the height of any layers stacked above it so it renders below the top
scalp.

Before/After:
https://b.corp.google.com/issues/583227885#attachment85204406

committer

Fri Nov 13 18:54:40 2026
tree
da5793b5a48433f3c8c6422fa94d5a5a21135d0d
parent
58424f983d27ba45331828209ab4d8d22de9b42b [ diff ] 
subresource_filter: Skip speculative kAdTagging check for non-HTTP/S

This CL gates speculative ActivationLevel::kDryRun pre-notification in
ContentSubresourceFilterThrottleManager on SchemeIsHTTPOrHTTPS() when
kAdTagging is enabled.

SafeBrowsingPageActivationThrottle and ProfileInteractionManager only
activate subresource filtering on HTTP/HTTPS URLs, disabling all other
root navigations (such as WebUI, extension, and file pages) at
WillProcessResponse. Previously, starting a non-HTTP/HTTPS navigation
with kAdTagging enabled still initialized a VerifiedRuleset::Handle and
posted a speculative check to the background ruleset task runner whose
reply on the default UI task queue could defer WillProcessResponse
during browser startup.

ContainerTiming: stop recording after the first input or scroll
📋 Metadata
commit
58424f983d27ba45331828209ab4d8d22de9b42b
[ log ]
author
Jose Dapena Paz <jdapena@igalia.com>
Fri Nov 13 18:54:08 2026
committer

Fri Nov 13 18:54:08 2026
tree
680290bbba86c3d71434af13e4a339d4b89f8642
parent
ebe243cbfdd16947a175aa5dc461b3e87b434c42 [ diff ] 
ContainerTiming: stop recording after the first input or scroll

The Container Timing spec now stops handling painted elements once the
window has dispatched an input or scroll event [1]. Container Timing
kept recording paints for the lifetime of the page.

ContainerTiming now tracks whether an input or scroll happened, and
stops recording paints afterwards: ContributesToContainerTiming()
returns false and OnElementPainted() ignores them. ElementTiming
forwards the same input and scroll signal LCP uses from
PaintTimingClient::OnInputOrScroll(). Element Timing is not affected.

Entries for paints committed before the input are created at paint
time, so they are still reported even if the frame is presented after
the input, as the spec note requires. This intentionally differs from
LCP, which drops candidates presented after the input.

[1] https://github.com/w3c/container-timing/pull/79

a45af4104a13b5807728b307a532b9292351394b
parent
69d3417717e0be9661b475183225fc0f3c22044e [ diff ] 
[Autofill] Don't log user fills while the actor task is paused

ActorAutofillManager logs every Autofill fill on a tab with an active
actor task as a critical action. The task stays active while it is
paused (e.g. the user took over), so forms the user fills themselves
during a takeover were recorded in chrome://history as Gemini's actions.

Track ActorTask::IsUnderUserControl() in ActorTaskInfo and skip logging
while the user controls the task. kWaitingOnUser is still under the
actor's control (the user can't interact with the tab), so fills there
keep being logged. Other actor-specific Autofill behavior is unchanged,
since IsTabInActorMode() still returns true during a pause.

f7ac267e50cd8c1d8992d648994f4ae552cb8db5 [ diff ] 
[SigninPrefs] Record primary account last sign-in time

Add per-account ChromeLastSigninTime preference to SigninPrefs
(SetChromeLastSigninTime / ClearAllChromeLastSigninTime /
GetChromeLastSigninTime) and record it behind the enabled-by-default
killswitch switches::kRecordChromeLastSigninTime:
- On primary account sign-in (PrimaryAccountChangeEvent::Type::kSet for
  ConsentLevel::kSignin) in
  PrimaryAccountManager::FirePrimaryAccountChanged (mirroring
  SetChromeLastSignoutTime under BUILDFLAG(ENABLE_DICE_SUPPORT)).
- On PrimaryAccountManager initialization:
  - When switches::kRecordChromeLastSigninTime is enabled, migrate
    pre-existing already signed-in profiles that do not yet have a
    stored ChromeLastSigninTime by stamping base::Time::Now() once
    without overwriting existing timestamps on subsequent startups.
  - When switches::kRecordChromeLastSigninTime is disabled,
    unconditionally clear any previously recorded ChromeLastSigninTime
    across all accounts in SigninPrefs via
    ClearAllChromeLastSigninTime() (backed by
    SigninPrefsAccessor::ClearPrefForAllAccounts in a single
    ScopedDictPrefUpdate).

Storing the timestamp in SigninPrefs ensures automatic per-account
cleanup when accounts are removed from Chrome and the cookie jar via
GAIAInfoUpdateService::RemoveAllAccountPrefsExcept.

27d548bd308ef94f77af127cdb98f1e0d9b5b4ff
parent
4e025b93fc87fff09ca5f5a780cc3db8d1c05030 [ diff ] 
context_hub: Refine Auto Tab Groups preferences when groups are created

"Create groups" now ends the Auto Tab Groups session via
`FinishTabGroupSession()`. If groups were created and the session's chat
history has a user turn, the service sends one
`AUTO_TAB_GROUPS_PREFERENCE_REFINEMENT` request carrying that history
and the effective preferences document, then clears the chat history and
unconfirmed suggestions either way.

The refined document is stored only when it is non-empty, within the
byte cap, different from the current one and not stale (the user did not
save or reset while the request was in flight). At most one refinement
is in flight; failures are dropped silently, no retries.

📋 Metadata

d8548bdbe39ffce525e89c68553006fccb17eae0
[ log ]
author
Rune Lillesveen <futhark@chromium.org>
Fri Nov 13 18:45:11 2026

tree
7dab328953bf3dd803ccb5cde71dd48df1417912
parent
3bc476ab5fa91f06dc95ddfebab4988ae06e63b5 [ diff ] 
Ignore CSS properties for effects passed into non-CSS animations

Effect detached from a CSSAnimation and passed in as an effect to an
Animation constructor did not mark the effect for ignoring CSS
properties like Animation::setEffect() does.

That caused crash issues because Animation would not update styles, yet
getKeyframes() would call StyleResolver::ComputeValue() on a dirty tree
which would cause UAF issues under certain circumstances involving
slot reassignment and container queries.

Call SetIgnoreCssTimingProperties() and SetIgnoreCSSKeyframes() in the
Animation constructor the same way we do in setEffect().

📋 Metadata

06baa9c2129f9b1aaab303f649780922b4629deb
[ log ]
author
Calder Kitagawa <ckitagawa@chromium.org>

tree
ff8fb5f3c8cb9ada47d59535254550540f830746
parent
ffaedcfb1ff5d7ef1bdad291677ff4a7ab5d72ef [ diff ] 
Use GlobalBrowserCollection for browser window lookups on Android

On Android, looking up a tab's browser window or iterating open browser
windows previously crossed JNI into Java on every call to build and copy
a fresh list of window pointers. During navigations with many subframes
and active extensions, repeating this JNI and Java allocation work on
the UI thread added unnecessary overhead during heavy page loads.

Because GlobalBrowserCollection already tracks all Android browser
windows and their activation order natively in C++, use it directly for
TabAndroid window lookups and share the cross-platform C++ iterator
implementation across Android and desktop, removing the now-unused
Android JNI iterator and enumerator classes.

Bypass-Check-License: Moved file

Introduces SuspiciousSiteTabHelper and SuspiciousSiteTabHelperDelegate
to manage the lifecycle, post-commit deferral, blocking-interstitial
precedence, 30-day local allowlist checks, and tab visibility suspension
for suspicious site warnings on iOS.

Also wires SafeBrowsingClientImpl::OnSuspiciousSiteWarningTriggered to
forward warning triggers to SuspiciousSiteTabHelper and attaches the
helper to non-incognito WebStates. User action handling and UMA metrics
will be added in a follow-up CL.

Design doc: go/suss-sites-ios-dd

750f5c4c3cf28fb25ac2503c1757a96c73918ed8 [ diff ] 
[webium] Prerender one toolbar for windows of any profile

Design: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1lzWRQwj2u4QItO-Wd3BbhoJbvc7dIxLFBpZeGumxEmw

This CL implements Phase 2 of the design. It keeps one spare prerendered
WebContents for windows of every profile, gated behind the
`TopChromeWebUIPrerenderingUnbound` param, which needs
`TopChromeWebUISharedRenderer`, `WebUIReloadButtonProfilePrewarming`,
and `WebUIReloadButtonPrewarmWebUIPreNavigate`. In Phase 1, each
regular profile keeps its own spare toolbar, and Incognito, Guest, and
other-profile windows get none.

An unbound-mode `InitialWebUIProfileService`, held by
TopChromeWebUIProfile, keeps one process-wide spare with no user
profile. It starts from the last active profile's theme, Glic and avatar
eligibility, and home button, read without binding. A new window of any
profile takes the spare and binds its profile, which recomputes
WebPreferences and renderer preferences.

The spare is dropped when the last normal window closes, and dropped
as stale at the next take after the native theme or touch mode changes.

Binding sends the toolbar only what differs from its starting state:

- Once the page has committed, the bound state goes on the frame
  channel ahead of the toolbar's resize and `WasShown`. The page stashes
  it if its script has not run yet. Before commit, the bound state also
  replaces the page's initial state. The page may still paint one frame
  before it applies the bound state; a follow-up CL holds the first
  paint until binding and adds a first-frame browser test.
- Theme CSS goes only when the theme differs. The page swaps it in
  through `document.adoptedStyleSheets`, and starts theme color updates
  only after binding.
- The page can add the Glic and avatar buttons after load.

Unchanged from Phase 1:

- With the param off, or without the shared renderer or PreNavigate,
  per-profile spares work as before.
- Each take still records
  `InitialWebUI.Toolbar.ProfilePrewarmTakeResult`.
- Refill timing and memory pressure handling.

Bug: b:573441656
Test: out/Default/unit_tests --gtest_filter='InitialWebUIProfileService*:InitialWebUIManager*:WebUIToolbarUI*:PrewarmHelper*'
Test: out/Default/browser_tests --gtest_filter='*InitialWebUI*:WebUiToolbarJsTest.*'

[iOS][Actor] Plumb async navigation confirmation callback for uncached origins

When a controlled tab encounters an uncached cross-origin navigation
response that does not require user confirmation, query an asynchronous
navigation confirmation callback instead of immediately failing closed.

- Invoke `ConfirmNavigationCallback` in
  `ActorOriginGatingCheckerDelegateIOS::OnNoVerdict` and caches the
  approved origins.
- Add `SetNavigationConfirmationCallback` on `ActorTask` and
  `ActorService` (with posted request/response hops to prevent
  re-entrancy), and wire `ActorEngine` to forward confirmation requests
  to its owning task.
- Expose `setNavigationConfirmationHandler:forTaskID:` on
  `GeminiActuationDelegate` and `GeminiActuationHandler` so the
  downstream actuation service can register the Origin Critic model
  check.

tree
f3ac26870f8988439292343a65dd8b96b3fc7c4b
parent
3486cd9496203769d02b1bbfbc9948c046051bf5 [ diff ] 
Set priority of BatteryLevelProviderWin to USER_VISIBLE

BatteryLevelProvider is used to log several metrics, and as input to
user_tuning::BatterySaverModeManager that can make user-visible changes
to Battery Saver Mode. The Windows implementation schedules blocking
tasks on a BEST_EFFORT task runner, which could starve.

Under the EnableBestEffortTaskInhibitingPolicy experiment, which
throttles BEST_EFFORT tasks aggressively in loading and input scenarios,
this leads to battery metrics being under-reported, especially during
navigation. It could also mean that during heavy navigation and
interaction, which discharges the battery faster, Battery Saver Mode is
blocked from engaging because the BatteryLevelProvider can't report the
battery drop.

The task runner should use USER_VISIBLE instead of BEST_EFFORT even if
that experiment doesn't ship, because the battery metrics are used to
judge the effects of scheduler changes, so should be reliable. By
definition BEST_EFFORT tasks may be arbitrarily delayed.

Also removes redundant ScopedBlockingCalls: the task runner already uses
the MayBlock() trait.

606fff9219fef63147daa5e54b7beae2ce6d9e95 [ diff ] 
PrivateAi: Fix re-entrant callback drop in ConnectionBasic

When `ConnectionBasic::CallOnDisconnect` handles a write failure, it
drains and invokes pending callbacks before clearing its
`on_disconnect_` state. If one of these callbacks synchronously
pipelines a new request, `Send()` enqueues it into
`pending_request_callbacks_` on the dead connection and it hangs
forever.

This CL fixes the re-entrancy bug by resetting `on_disconnect_` prior to
invoking the pending callbacks, causing re-entrant `Send()` calls to be
immediately rejected.

cefddca92065d0978b1a407faaacfefec6b0f970 [ diff ] 
[TabUI Virtualization] Let editor callers pass the data provider

Each surface that opens the tab list editor now builds its own data
provider and hands it in, instead of the editor picking one from its
layout and component ID.

The editor used to build a grouped provider for every grouped caller
except the archived tabs dialog, and a flat provider that filtered on
the tabs passed to show(). So a caller had to know about the component
ID check, and the flat filter also showed every tab in the first tab's
group. For the item picker, that added tabs it had filtered out as not
eligible.

Now the tab switcher's "Select tabs" editor passes a grouped provider,
the group dialog passes a flat one for the open group, and the item
picker passes one limited to its eligible tabs. The archived tabs dialog
passes nothing and stays on the legacy path, since saved tab groups
aren't supported by providers yet. Each caller owns and destroys its
provider. Callers can't reuse their own list's provider because the
editor stops it when it hides. All of this is behind
TabUiDataVirtualization, which is off by default.

[blink] Move Fast API range and restricted float checks to C++

Pass double (for [EnforceRange] integer parameters/setters) and raw
float/double (for restricted floating-point parameters/setters) directly
into Blink [NoAllocDirectCall] callbacks instead of setting V8's
CTypeInfo::Flags::kEnforceRangeBit or kIsRestrictedBit.

Previously, V8 emitted inline generated range/finiteness checks along
with a regular FunctionCallbackInfo slow-call fallback per Fast API
call site, while Blink's C++ EnforceRange<T> called libm trunc() on
x86-64 builds without -msse4.1.

Introduce TryEnforceRange<T> using exact IEEE-754 double open-interval
bounds and static_cast<T> (with zero trunc() call), share it between
regular EnforceRange<T> and the new EnforceRangeForNoAllocDirectCall<T>,
and add CheckRestrictedFloatingPointForNoAllocDirectCall<T> with cold
NOINLINE exception helpers so HandleScope and ExceptionState stay off
the Fast API hot path. Also pass --no-turbolev in virtual/fast-api so
the suite exercises Turboshaft's FastApiCall lowering.

TAG=agy
CONV=b0980797-5c88-428c-9e9c-8f8535f2bbc4

Bug: None
Test: blink_unittests --gtest_filter=NativeValueTraitsImplTest.*
Test: blink_web_tests virtual/fast-api/bindings/fast-api-attributes.html

[Perf] Lazily initialize mObservers in StripLayoutTab

Lazily creates mObservers in StripLayoutTab to eliminate eager
allocation on dormant, background, and inactive tabs.

Changes:
- Converts mObservers to @Nullable and defers instantiation.
- Instantiates mObservers upon first addObserver() invocation.
- Null-guards removeObserver() and observer iteration.
- Saves ~48 bytes per tab instance in ART heap.
- Preserves observer notification semantics and order.

Why:
StripLayoutTab allocates mObservers up front on every tab, but it is
usually empty. It is created when each tab is created, but not used
until the tab is shown. Nothing in production fills it: the only caller
of StripLayoutTab.addObserver() is test-only. Saves about 48 B per tab,
or about 47 KB with 1,000 tabs open (rough estimate).

Created via (line numbers before this change):
  1. ChromeTabbedActivity
  2. LayoutManagerChromeTablet
  3. StripLayoutHelperManager
  4. StripLayoutHelper.createStripTab()
  5. StripLayoutTab
     chrome/android/java/src/org/chromium/chrome/browser/compositor/overlays/strip/StripLayoutTab.java:241

Uses of mObservers in StripLayoutTab (line numbers before this
change):
  1. StripLayoutTab.mObservers initializer (allocation)
     chrome/android/java/src/org/chromium/chrome/browser/compositor/overlays/strip/StripLayoutTab.java:241
  2. StripLayoutTab.addObserver(Observer) (first write, test-only)
     chrome/android/java/src/org/chromium/chrome/browser/compositor/overlays/strip/StripLayoutTab.java:541
  3. StripLayoutTab.removeObserver(Observer) (removal)
     chrome/android/java/src/org/chromium/chrome/browser/compositor/overlays/strip/StripLayoutTab.java:548
  4. StripLayoutTab.onVisibilityChanged(boolean) (read)
     chrome/android/java/src/org/chromium/chrome/browser/compositor/overlays/strip/StripLayoutTab.java:749

3c4f4afc735a9bbd491760647b6b01d2c96d5cce [ diff ] 
[TabUI] Clear the tab list editor's selection when it opens

Opening "Select tabs" now starts with nothing selected, so the select
all button says "Select all" instead of "Deselect all".

The editor kept its selection between sessions. After selecting all tabs
in a group and closing them, the closed tabs' IDs stayed selected. The
tab group dialog sets up the editor menu only once, so the button kept
its old "Deselect all" label the next time the editor opened, with
nothing selected. The tab switcher's editor refreshes its menu on each
open, but the select all check compares counts, so leftover IDs could
still flip the label there.

Clearing on open rather than on close lets the button check against the
new list of tabs. Callers that preselect tabs do so after the editor
opens, so their selection is kept.

[content] Fix inaccurate nested-message-loop comments and tests

Win32 window-management calls in UpdateLegacyWin() (::CreateWindowEx(),
::SetParent(), ::SetWindowPos()) synchronously dispatch window messages
to the WndProc rather than spinning a nested message loop, and native
OS/IME calls (such as SetInputScope()) entered inside DoWorkImpl() do
not pump Chromium tasks or Mojo IPCs (see content/browser/SECURITY.md).

Preserve all existing defensive WeakPtr, CHECK, and
ScopedInputDispatchPin guards (including crrev.com/c/8527720,
crrev.com/c/8620676, and crrev.com/c/8652580) while:
- Updating inaccurate nested-message-loop comments in WebContentsImpl,
  RenderFrameHostImpl, WebContentsView, WebContentsViewAura,
  RenderWidgetHostViewAura, LegacyRenderWidgetHostHWND, and unit tests.
- Removing synthetic base::RunLoop(kNestableTasksAllowed) and PostTask()
  harnesses from WebContentsViewAuraReentrantDestructionTest so the
  tests exercise synchronous re-entrancy directly.

parent
d4312c4d35115d6329dfc8a6669667c648ff42ef [ diff ] 
[glic] Record FRE state on InvokeStarted/InvokeTerminated

Invocation outcomes cannot currently be sliced by whether the user had
consented: the Glic.Fre.* histograms are profile scoped and carry no
invocation id, so a first-run attempt that ends with the panel closed
looks the same as a returning user's. Attach the FRE state the
invocation inherited when it started to InvokeStarted:

- FreStatusAtStart: the profile's prefs::FreStatus
- FreOverride / FreWaitMode: what the caller asked for
- RequiresClientInvoke: whether the web client must receive a payload
- WaitsForFre: whether the invocation blocks on consent
  (GlicInvokeHandler::NeedsFreCompletion())

Captured next to ClientLoadStateAtStart, before any task runs, and
absent for invocations rejected before they start. InvokeTerminated
also carries FreStatusAtStart and WaitsForFre so outcomes can be
sliced without a join.

a732439b3640f038522c1207e08e01d9a82a794c
parent
40cbe0b8b9254182736595b0dac5117c2e645aa8 [ diff ] 
[iOS] Send INP data on visibility change

During cross-site navigations, the renderer process sends the
visibilitychange event to the browser with a delay of 15ms to 250ms,
depending on how busy the renderer is. To account for this delay, we now
log INP data for the previous navigation instead of the current one,
effectively deferring the logging by one navigation. Note that for
same-site navigations, the message is still reliably received before
didFinishNavigation.

Since we have the data at the last possible moment, we ensure that the
data (interaction count, interaction data) are correct and accurate.

`visibilitychange` is called for both navigation and going into the tab
switcher. This mean that if we close the tab from the tab switcher, we
will already have the data to calculate the INP.

[Capture] Release SCStream on close and clear picker state on lock
📋 Metadata

c76fb7573d84485994011bfbd63a2dbaf9f67412

author

tree
0a18adc163bc5bdef4d85ea634cceb68103d913d
parent
02fd8ca2ff90778945dbb8473a33c767b0cba323 [ diff ] 
[Capture] Release SCStream on close and clear picker state on lock

NativeScreenCapturePickerMac::Close() previously retained the strong
SCStream* reference in stream_to_id_map_ until the 60-second
ScheduleCleanup() timer fired. Only the SCContentFilter* in sessions_
is needed for applyConstraints() restarts, because CreateDevice()
allocates a new SCStream. Retaining the stopped SCStream* for 60
seconds kept the ScreenCaptureKit session alive in replayd after
capture stopped.

Erase SCStream* entries from stream_to_id_map_ immediately in Close()
and UpdateStreamMap(), and observe ScreenlockObserver::OnScreenLocked()
to clear cached sessions_ and deactivate the picker immediately when
the screen locks.

Also close ordering gaps that could keep state alive anyway:
- The device posts UpdateStreamMap(), so it can run after Close() or
  OnScreenLocked(). Ignore it unless the session is still active.
- Close() reaches the picker after OnScreenLocked() on a lock. Make
  ScheduleCleanup() skip sessions that no longer exist instead of
  recreating them with a new 60-second timer.
- A device launch can call CreateDevice() after the session was
  dropped. Return null instead of recreating the session, so the
  source is not left active with no Close() to follow.
- Closing one session no longer hides a picker that is still waiting
  for the user to choose a surface.

608aa230164204aabbbd5db9a4a7ec1b1eae0d26
parent
79e4942ce0a40f974edc1481ebcb11edf2b8b43b [ diff ] 
Migrate DevTools TabModelList callers to profile and global APIs

`TabModelList` is being migrated from unscoped global accessors to
explicit profile-scoped and global APIs to support multi-profile on
Android.

Migrate `CloseAndroidTabsForProfile()` in
`DevToolsBrowserContextManager` to `TabModelList::ForEachAll()` using
`ForceCloseAllTabs()` and `GetAllTabs()` so disposable non-primary
DevTools OTR profiles close their tabs across both isolated and shared
`TabModel`s. `TargetHandlerAndroid::CreateTarget` uses
`TabModelList::ForEach(default_profile, ...)` to select the default
profile's standard `TabModel` while preserving zero-window
`CreateBrowserWindow` under `kEnforceIncognitoIsolation`. Also migrate
`GlobalConfirmInfoBar`, `DevToolsManagerDelegateAndroid`, and the
DevTools browsertests—which operate across all browser windows and
targets process-wide—to `TabModelList::ForEachAll()`,
`AddGlobalObserver()`, and `RemoveGlobalObserver()`.

[thin_webview] Remove UseSurfaceViewForThinWebView feature flag

UseSurfaceViewForThinWebView has been enabled by default since M152
(crrev.com/c/8144401), and its chrome://flags entry reaches its
expiry milestone in M160.

Remove the feature, its JNI query and its chrome://flags entry.
CompositorViewImpl now picks the view type from the constraints
alone: TextureView when the view needs opacity support, SurfaceView
otherwise.

[Manifest] Add ParseArray/ParseObject helpers and simplify field parsers

- Add ParseArray and ParseObject helpers to replace double JSON lookups
  (Get() followed by GetArray() or GetJSONObject()) and standardize
  type-error reporting across 15 methods.

  ScopeExtensionTypeMap array in favor of comparing against "origin".

  ParseFileHandlerAccept by validating the already-extracted String in
  ValidateFileHandlerAcceptExtension().
- Deduplicate field extraction in ParseTranslations and reuse
  ParseLocaleKey() in ParseLocalizedField().

bc2eef4e9b62d294d059012885bae2d013539791
parent
c37b8a96329ed7270324289bd353826306e47aaf [ diff ] 
[a11y] Plumb selection offset types to WebAXObject::SetSelection

BrowserAccessibilityManager::SetSelection(AXRange) starts from
AXPositions that know whether they are text positions or tree
positions, but only bare integer offsets were sent to the renderer,
forcing WebAXObject::SetSelection() to infer what each offset meant.

This CL populates AXActionData::anchor_offset_type and
focus_offset_type in BrowserAccessibilityManager::SetSelection() (while
keeping a TODO for the remaining affinity work) and plumbs them through
RenderAccessibilityImpl::PerformAction(), ui::AXActionTarget, and
BlinkAXActionTarget::SetSelection() into WebAXObject::SetSelection().

AX-Relnotes: n/a.

tree
1c1bd464e4b4cef0c2157da6aa962acd4a7ba71a
parent
69d1fed18b80b3a56dc4948efa78ec4dab391291 [ diff ] 
Release AppLaunchDrawBlocker draw blocks on destroy

- destroy() now removes the pre-draw listeners and clears all draw-block
  flags, and the recreation block gets a 2000 ms fallback timeout.
- Why destroy() fixes it: each block is a pre-draw listener on the
  window's view tree that cancels draws until its flag is cleared by
  that Activity's first compositor swap. If the Activity is destroyed
  before that swap (e.g. a density change right after a rotation) and
  the window is preserved for the next instance, the old listener stays
  and cancels every later draw. destroy() removes the listener; clearing
  the flags covers a listener that could not be removed.
- Why this was not needed before: until crrev.com/c/8254965 the blocks
  were aimed at cold start, where a leftover listener normally goes away
  with its window. The recreation block is the first that routinely runs
  on a window that may be reused.
- The timeout is only a backstop for a first swap that never arrives.

Demo: These demos use a synthetic repro - an adb script forces rapid
display rotation, size and density changes on a Pixel Tablet emulator to
mimic the issue, so they do not show real user interaction:

Before: http://screencast/cast/NjI2OTY2OTEyMzAzMTA0MHwxZGQxMmFhOC1iMA
After: http://screencast/cast/NTQzNTA0NDM0MzU3ODYyNHxiZmNmZTE2NC01ZA

positions leave the card list right. One end-to-end test per layout

and checks only the resulting card order.

in a group, where the move arrives for a tab that isn't listed yet.

tree
59e8ef1e275714a985fe4aefe1dd5448dc4bc405
parent
6378f2dd595dcaa501e26669df907aaac3a159bc [ diff ] 
[Extensions] Fire only "enable" when enabled during browser startup

ExtensionService::Init() can enable a disabled extension before it
signals ExtensionSystem::ready(), for example when policy no longer
forces it disabled, or when its only disable reason is an interrupted
reload. The ExtensionRegistrar::EnableExtension() call for the enable
notifies RuntimeAPI that the extension loaded, which posts
runtime.onExtensionLoaded with reason "startup", and then that it was
enabled, which posts another with reason "enable". The extension then
gets two onExtensionLoaded events for one load, but the proposal[1] says
only one should fire.

After this change, an extension that is enabled during browser startup
gets one runtime.onExtensionLoaded with reason "enable" (since "enable"
takes precedence over "browser_update" and "startup" load reasons).

runtime.onEnabled still fires, and runtime.onInstalled with reason
"chrome_update" still fires after a browser update.

[1]:
https://github.com/w3c/webextensions/blob/main/proposals/runtime_on_load_on_enabled_events.md

36e36c6768c5aace937df5e0e87195c82fb002e5
parent
10f09c42517e93a4e10e0f79c808128314a183ab [ diff ] 
[e2e] Add kViewportFitCoverAndSafeAreaInsetTop UseCounter and UKM

Previously, Blink recorded `kViewportFitCover` and
`kCSSEnvironmentVariable_SafeAreaInsetTop` independently, preventing
page-level intersection analysis in UMA histograms to identify sites
that opt into top edge-to-edge rendering while also accounting for top
safe-area insets. This change adds
`kViewportFitCoverAndSafeAreaInsetTop`, records it on the outermost main
frame regardless of whether `viewport-fit=cover` or
`env(safe-area-inset-top)` is evaluated first, and adds it to the UKM
UseCounter allowlist.

UKM Eligibility:
- Subset of existing UKM-allowed counters: Both constituent counters
  (`kViewportFitCover` and `kCSSEnvironmentVariable_SafeAreaInsetTop`)
  are already in `GetAllowedUkmFeatures()`. Recording their intersection
  (`AND` condition on the outermost main frame) exposes no new
  page-level signal to UKM.
- Low volume (<1% of page loads): `kViewportFitCover` occurs on ~0.09%
  of page loads in UMA. As a strict mathematical subset (intersection)
  of `kViewportFitCover`, `kViewportFitCoverAndSafeAreaInsetTop` is
  guaranteed to remain well below the 1% UKM UseCounter threshold.

ChromeVox: Enter overview before enabling ChromeVox in overview test

SpokenFeedbackTest.EnableChromeVoxOnOverviewMode was disabled in
crbug.com/40831399. Running the test locally reproduces a deterministic
timeout waiting for "Click me":
  xvfb-run -a ./out/Default/browser_tests \
    --gtest_filter="*SpokenFeedbackTest.DISABLED_EnableChromeVoxOnOverviewMode*" \
    --gtest_also_run_disabled_tests

The test checks that enabling ChromeVox while in window overview mode
does not exit overview mode. However, it queued the page load and
overview toggle for deferred speech replay while starting ChromeVox
synchronously beforehand, so ChromeVox started before the page load and
overview toggle and its startup speech raced the "Click me" wait.

Load the test page and enter overview mode before starting ChromeVox,
drop the old pre-Tab ", window" wait that waited for entering overview
after ChromeVox was already running, assert that overview mode is still
active before pressing Tab, check for both the overview window item and
the "Press Ctrl plus W to close." prompt after pressing Tab, and
re-enable the test. Passes 60/60 local repeat runs across all 4
ManifestV2 and ManifestV3 normal and guest variants, with 40/40
standard runs and 20/20 runs under taskset -c 0-1:
  xvfb-run -a ./out/Default/browser_tests \
    --gtest_filter="*SpokenFeedbackTest.EnableChromeVoxOnOverviewMode*" \
    --gtest_repeat=10 --test-launcher-bot-mode --test-launcher-jobs=4

Re-enable ChromeVox live status off browser test

ChromeVoxLiveRegionsTest.LiveStatusOff was disabled in 2019 under
crbug.com/40619839 for flakiness. That original 2019 flake did not
reproduce locally today once the test setup below was repaired; the
only local evidence it is gone is the 25/25 passing repeat runs below.

Today, the test fails deterministically at base:
  xvfb-run -a ./out/Default/browser_tests \
    --gtest_filter=ChromeVoxLiveRegionsTest.DISABLED_LiveStatusOff \
    --gtest_also_run_disabled_tests

In 2022, the async runWithLoadedTree refactor (crrev.com/c/3556126)
renamed the loaded tree variable to rootNode while leaving root.find,
causing an immediate ReferenceError. Once root is restored, the test
times out waiting for the initial 'bb' value to be spoken: because the
text input was the first focusable element in the document, page load
completion automatically focused the empty input and created its text
edit handler before the test body ran, and calling focus and clicking
the parent container back-to-back raced between the focus event and the
two-character value update ('' to 'bb'), which ChromeVox does not speak
as typing echo in an already-focused text field.

Restore the root variable name, add a leading paragraph so initial page
load does not focus the empty text input, and focus the input once its
initial value change event fires so focus speech announces 'bb' before
the second click updates the value to 'bba' and verifies that with
aria-live="off" only the typed character 'a' (and not 'bba') is spoken.
Passes 25/25 local repeat runs (20/20 standard runs and 5/5 runs under
taskset -c 0-1):
  xvfb-run -a ./out/Default/browser_tests \
    --gtest_filter=ChromeVoxLiveRegionsTest.LiveStatusOff \
    --gtest_repeat=20 --gtest_break_on_failure

ChromeVox: Check message IDs at build time via union type

Previously, Msgs.getMsg() and ChromeVox message ID tables accepted
arbitrary strings, so typos or accidental string removals from
chromevox_strings.grdp were only caught at runtime.

Generate a ChromeVoxResourceMessageId union type from
_locales/en/messages.json.gz at build time and type Msgs.getMsg(),
Msgs.getMsgWithCount(), and static message ID tables across ChromeVox
MV2 and MV3. Runtime-resolved DOM msgid attributes and output @tokens
use Msgs.getDynamicMsg().

Roll Arm AFDO from 160.0.8156.0_pre1741658_rc-r1-merged to 160.0.8159.0_pre1743830_rc-r1-merged

This CL may cause a small binary size increase, roughly proportional
to how long it's been since our last AFDO profile roll. For larger
increases (around or exceeding 100KB), please file go/crostc-bug.

Please note that, despite rolling to chrome/android, this profile is
used for both Linux and Android.

If this roll has caused a breakage, revert this CL and set the roller
to dry run mode using the controls here:
https://autoroll.skia.org/r/afdo-arm-chromium
Please CC c-compiler-chrome@google.com,gbiv@google.com,mobiletc-prebuild@google.com on the revert to ensure that a human
is aware of the problem.

To file a bug in Chromium Main: https://bugs.chromium.org/p/chromium/issues/entry

To report a problem with the AutoRoller itself, please file a bug:

[omnibox] Test app menu fullscreen with the full webui popup focused.

On ChromeOS with the Full WebUI omnibox, opening the NTP (which focuses
the omnibox) and clicking "Full screen" in the 3-dot menu left the popup
focused. Immersive fullscreen then kept top chrome revealed over the
page, so fullscreen didn't show the whole page.

crrev.com/c/8717429 already fixed this: BrowserView::PrepareFullscreen()
now blurs the Full popup on ChromeOS. Its tests enter fullscreen
through IDC_FULLSCREEN and the Fullscreen key. The app menu path is
different: the command runs while the menu is open, and
OmniboxPopupFullPresenter::AppMenuClosed(), which can refocus the
omnibox, runs afterwards.

This CL adds FullscreenFromAppMenuBlursFocusedOmnibox, which clicks the
real "Full screen" button in the app menu and checks that the omnibox is
blurred, the popup is closed, and top chrome is hidden.

The test fails with the PrepareFullscreen() blur from
crrev.com/c/8717429 removed and passes at ToT (verified on
linux-chromeos).

99e52b05ff5eebb4d156ec9d4ff3cfad349af4d9 [ diff ] 
[ash] auth: look the auth session's user up by account id

ShowAuthDialog() took the account id off the session controller, then
asked the UserManager separately for the active user to build the
UserContext from, while reading the ephemeral flag for the account id.
Two lookups for one user, which agree only as long as nothing moves
between them.

The user is now found by that same account id, so the context and the
ephemeral flag describe the same person by construction. It also drops
an unchecked dereference of the active user.

#ai-assisted

[Omnibox] Blur and close OmniboxPopupLocationBarHost on focus loss

Losing views focus on the embedded WebView, including when the window
deactivates, blurs the omnibox as for OmniboxViewViews and runs
OmniboxPopupViewFullWebUI::BlurOmnibox(). On deactivation,
LocationBarView (the Full WebUI focus proxy) replaces the WebView as the
stored focus view, so FocusManager's restore on reactivation refocuses
the omnibox through it. That refocus runs in a posted task, so a click
that reactivated the window takes focus first.

Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter='*OmniboxPopup*'
Test: interactive_ui_tests --gtest_filter='OmniboxPopupLocationBarHost*'

d25c252adeb3d5ad45a2b5cce03c5d8c63a70215
parent
d2319eafafcc6a6b888ed3277613e641ef4abe75 [ diff ] 
[clang-plugin] Add excluded_pointer_types to the container config

Some pointer types never point to PartitionAlloc memory, e.g. Win32
HANDLE/HWND, Vulkan VkSemaphore or Objective-C id. raw_ptr<T> gives
them no protection, and for some of them raw_ptr<T> doesn't compile.
This adds a check_raw_ptr_in_container option listing the types that
the check should skip. The list is empty by default.

A field is skipped when any template argument of its container, as
written in the source, is a typedef whose fully-qualified name is
listed. False negatives are acceptable, but a false positive on such
a type can't be fixed:
- Aliases are not resolved, so HMODULE must be listed separately from
  HINSTANCE.
- Any listed argument skips the field, e.g. std::tuple<HANDLE, T*>.
- Only the outermost written specialization is inspected, e.g.
  base::flat_set<HANDLE>; alias templates aren't expanded.

Bug: 581866564
Doc: https://bit.ly/miracleptr-in-container

[iOS] Keep Reading Mode active across uncommitted navigations

ReaderModeTabHelper previously reset eligibility decisions and flushed
deactivation metrics in DidStartNavigation, and deactivated Reading Mode
in DidFinishNavigation without checking HasCommitted().

Upcoming ios/web fixes ensure uncommitted navigations such as 204/205
responses (crrev.com/c/8786022) and downloads (crrev.com/c/8785741)
reliably dispatch DidFinishNavigation with HasCommitted() false while
keeping the existing document active.

Defer eligibility/metric resets and Reading Mode deactivation until a
cross-document navigation commits in DidFinishNavigation so uncommitted
navigations leave Reading Mode and distillability state intact.

[Direct Offers] Add loyalty cards to the autofillPrivate API

Adds `autofillPrivate.getLoyaltyCardList()` and the
`autofillPrivate.onLoyaltyCardsChanged` event, so that the "Offers and
loyalty cards" settings page can list the user's Google Wallet loyalty
cards the same way the other autofill settings pages get their data.

Each LoyaltyCardEntry contains the card's id, merchant name and card
number, and a link to Google Wallet. Sorting is left to the settings
page. For now every card links to the Wallet passes page, the same
fallback used for other Wallet passes such as vehicle registrations; a
follow-up will use each card's pass_view_url when available.

AutofillPrivateEventRouter now observes ValuablesDataManager, honoring
kAutofillPrivateEventRouterObserveOnDemand.

Design: go/offers-and-loyalty-cards-settings-leaf

[RTS] Enable chromium_tests.resultdb_module on rts-complement-runner

The runner uploads its results under the CQ builder's variant so LUCI
Analysis can exonerate known flaky failures, but the CQ builders use the
structured test IDs from this experiment and the runner still uploads
the legacy ninja:// ones. Without the same IDs there's no history to
match and known flakes end up as false acceptances.
