c879d4722e2a0915ba22a7fd455075e1d6fecb15 [ diff ] 
Return actual counts in WebFeatureHistogramTester::WaitForCountsAtLeast

WebFeatureHistogramTester::WaitForCountsAtLeast() is documented to
return the last observed feature counts, but ended with
`return expected;`. As a result, `ExpectCounts()` always passed once
the wait loop exited, masking over-counts or unexpected non-zero counts
on zero-expected features.

Return `counts` instead of `expected`, add browser tests covering
matching counts, over-counts, and unexpected feature counts, and update
three stale `LocalNetworkAccessCountersBrowserTest` expectations that
navigate from a public HTTPS page to a loopback server and record
`kAddressSpacePublicSecureContextNavigatedToLoopbackV2`.

Fixed: 585276537

d20deb85a3a3249deba624e33c6ba42051f0d40e [ diff ] 
[SystemWebViewShell] Add JsBridgeActivity for JS-Java bridge benchmarking

Embedded WebView apps rely heavily on WebView.addJavascriptInterface and
WebViewCompat.addWebMessageListener across multi-step navigations, where
leaked GinJavaBridge wrappers, JS listener closures, or retained frame
contexts directly inflate renderer memory. Existing WebView Shell
activities only load a single static URL and do not exercise JS-Java
bridge lifecycles across navigations.

Add JsBridgeActivity (subclassing ManuallyTriggeredStartupActivity) to
SystemWebViewShell to support an upcoming Crossbench WebView memory
benchmark. Navigating to https://origina.webviewshell.test/start drives
a deterministic, hermetic sequence of 20 synthetic HTTPS navigations
(overridable via the "steps" intent extra) served via
shouldInterceptRequest, alternating same-origin and cross-origin
transitions, same-frame document.open() clears, and child iframe
attach/detach while exercising both bridge APIs before settling on
about:blank.

TAG=agy
CONV=c6e847ae-a52d-4d33-a06a-f5c1f08dc7e7

2692652129505a80e966acdcb91bf7c0eec49337
parent
8d2acb1c4968c54b30550b77e19a98c60893b783 [ diff ] 
[Clank/Omnibox] Show the Omnibox on slow loads and on hidden tabs

In the ClankOmniboxMinimization experiment, a same-origin navigation can
skip the load lock, so the Omnibox stays hidden while the page loads.
Before this change, the Omnibox stayed hidden for the full load, and the
user saw no progress on a slow load. Also, if the tab went to the
background during such a navigation and came back before the navigation
finished, the lock was missing.

Now a navigation that skipped the lock takes it in two cases:

- It does not finish within fallback_show_after_ms. If such navigations
  overlap, the delay counts from the start of the first one, even if
  that one finishes first. If all of them finish, the timer stops.
- The tab is hidden while the navigation is in flight.

Content usually finishes a replaced navigation before the new one
starts, so the timer restarts from the new navigation.

The lock is then released as usual, getLoadDelayMs() after the last
navigation finishes. If fallback_show_after_ms is 0 or less, the
navigation takes the normal lock at start, the same as with the feature
off.

Reland "[TTC] Add PointerContextCapturer for get_pointer"

This is a reland of commit 9ffd399ae56d3f6c6163a05cdba7284f20367935.

The original CL was reverted in https://crrev.com/c/8806587 because
PointerContextCapturerScaleBrowserTest.CapturesScreenshotAndPageContent
failed at scale factor 1.5 on android-14-arm64-rel and other 420dpi
Android CI builders (1.50000012 != 1.5).

On Android, DisplayUtil.forcedScaleUpDisplayMetrics() implements
--force-device-scale-factor by scaling the display's native density
(density *= forced / density), which rounds off by 1 ULP for 1.5 on a
2.625-density (420dpi) display.

Changes from Patchset 1 (baseline): Use ASSERT_NEAR and EXPECT_NEAR with
a 0.001f tolerance instead of ASSERT_EQ and EXPECT_EQ when checking the
device scale factor in PointerContextCapturerScaleBrowserTest.

Original change's description:
> [TTC] Add PointerContextCapturer for get_pointer
>
> To answer "what is this?", get_pointer needs two things from the page:
> a screenshot to crop the image of the pointed-at spot from, and the
> page content to find the element at that spot in.
> PointerContextCapturer fetches both with a single PageContextFetcher
> request, so that they describe the same moment and the element found
> is the one in the image.
>
> The screenshot is the viewport at its natural size, barely compressed
> since the image made from it is compressed again, and the page content
> is extracted in actionable mode, which has geometry in device pixels;
> the capturer reports the device scale factor, which then converts the
> user's DIP positions for both.
>
> The user is waiting on a spoken answer and captures can stall, e.g.
> while the window is occluded, so the capturer gives up after a timeout
> chosen by its caller and reports kToolTimeout. Failures are reported
> as the ToolResponse to answer the tool call with, using the codes
> get_screenshot uses; the mapping from fetch errors moves to
> ToScreenshotErrorCode() to be shared.
>
> Nothing uses the capturer yet; the tool is assembled in a future CL,
> which also checks that the page's URL may be captured, as
> get_screenshot does.
>
> Design: go/ttc-get-pointer (CL C2 of the plan)
>
>     --gtest_filter=*PointerContextCapturer*
>
> Bug: 571268337
> Test: browser_tests / android_browsertests
> Change-Id: If18ff14d77cc8c56f41b27d3b5fdfee374c433d0
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8795395
> Reviewed-by: Kevin McNee <mcnee@chromium.org>
> Commit-Queue: James Cook <jamescook@chromium.org>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1743591}

Bug: 571268337

[UndoTabClosures] Rename SAVED_GROUPS snapshot kind to ARCHIVED

Rename UndoClosureSnapshot.ClosureKind.SAVED_GROUPS, forSavedGroups, and
UndoClosureSnapshotFactory.captureSavedGroups to ARCHIVED, forArchived,
and captureArchived. Now that hidden synced tab groups are captured
under ClosureKind.TAB_MODEL, ARCHIVED more clearly distinguishes
closures of archived tabs and archived tab groups from the Archived Tabs
dialog.
